import unittest
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import patch
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
from dispatch.web.app import app
from dispatch.db import init_db, save_clip, update_clip_media

client = TestClient(app)

class TestWebDashboard(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_web_dashboard(self):
        init_db()

        # 1. Test homepage
        res = client.get("/")
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200, f"Expected 200, got {res.status_code}")
        self.assertIn("Dispatch", res.text)

        # 2. Test status endpoint
        res = client.get("/api/status")
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
        data = res.json()
        self.assertIn("disk_free_gb", data)
        self.assertIn("ready_clips", data)

        # 3. Create a test clip and verify list endpoint
        clip_id = save_clip(
            session_id=None,
            chunk_id=None,
            start_time=1.0,
            end_time=25.0,
            title="Web test clip title",
            hook="Opening test",
            description="Description",
            hashtags="#Test",
            virality_score=85,
            layout_mode="fit_blur"
        )

        res = client.get("/api/clips")
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
        clips_data = res.json()
        clip_ids = [c["id"] for c in clips_data["ready"]]
        self.assertIn(clip_id, clip_ids, f"Clip {clip_id} not found in ready list")

        # 4. Test approval
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            source = Path(tmp) / "source.mp4"
            output = Path(tmp) / "rendered.mp4"
            source.write_bytes(b"test source")

            def render_for_test(**kwargs):
                output.write_bytes(b"test rendered media")
                update_clip_media(kwargs["clip_id"], str(output), None, kwargs["layout_mode"])
                return {"video_path": str(output)}

            with patch("dispatch.web.app.resolve_clip_source_path", return_value=source), \
                 patch("dispatch.web.app.render_clip", side_effect=render_for_test), \
                 patch("dispatch.ingestion.validator.probe_video", return_value=(True, {"duration": 24.0}, None)):
                res = client.post(f"/api/clips/{clip_id}/approve", json={
                    "title": "Approved Title",
                    "hashtags": "#Approved",
                    "publish_mode": "private",
                    "platforms": "youtube,instagram"
                })
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
        self.assertEqual(res.json()["status"], "success")

        # 5. Test pipeline jobs endpoint
        res = client.get("/api/pipeline/jobs")
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
        self.assertIn("jobs", res.json())

        # 6. Test integrations status endpoint
        res = client.get("/api/integrations/status")
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
        self.assertIn("youtube", res.json())


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()


============================================================
FILE: tests\test_youtube_auth_pairing.py
============================================================
import json
import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch

from dispatch.sync.pairing_codes import (
    PairingCodeError,
    consume_pairing_code,
    issue_pairing_code,
    reset_pairing_codes_for_tests,
)
from scripts import auth_youtube


class YouTubeAuthTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        self.temp_dir = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(dir=Path(__file__).resolve().parent)
        self.root = Path(self.temp_dir.name)
        (self.root / "client_secrets.json").write_text("{}", encoding="utf-8")
        self.token_file = self.root / "youtube_token.json"
        self.token_file.write_text("old-token", encoding="utf-8")
        self.credentials = MagicMock(refresh_token="refresh", to_json=lambda: '{"token":"new"}')
        self.flow = MagicMock()
        self.flow.run_local_server.return_value = self.credentials

    def tearDown(self):
        self.temp_dir.cleanup()

    def _run_auth(self, channels_result):
        service = MagicMock()
        service.channels.return_value.list.return_value.execute.return_value = channels_result
        with patch.object(auth_youtube, "ROOT_DIR", self.root), \
             patch.object(auth_youtube, "CLIENT_SECRETS_FILE", self.root / "client_secrets.json"), \
             patch.object(auth_youtube, "TOKEN_FILE", self.token_file), \
             patch("google_auth_oauthlib.flow.InstalledAppFlow.from_client_secrets_file", return_value=self.flow), \
             patch("googleapiclient.discovery.build", return_value=service):
            return auth_youtube.run_authentication()

    def test_token_is_saved_only_after_channel_verification(self):
        with self.assertRaises(RuntimeError):
            self._run_auth({"items": []})
        self.assertEqual(self.token_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), "old-token")

        channel = self._run_auth({"items": [{"id": "channel-id", "snippet": {"title": "Channel"}}]})
        self.assertEqual(channel["channel_id"], "channel-id")
        self.assertEqual(json.loads(self.token_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8")), {"token": "new"})


class PairingCodeTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        reset_pairing_codes_for_tests()

    def tearDown(self):
        reset_pairing_codes_for_tests()

    def test_generated_code_is_strictly_four_numeric_digits(self):
        """Verify that every generated code contains exactly four numeric digits."""
        for _ in range(50):
            code, expiry = issue_pairing_code()
            self.assertEqual(len(code), 4, f"Generated code {code} is not 4 digits")
            self.assertTrue(code.isdigit(), f"Generated code {code} is not all digits")
            self.assertEqual(expiry, 300)

    def test_code_with_leading_zeros_accepted(self):
        """Verify codes with leading zeros, such as '0047', are properly accepted."""
        with patch("secrets.randbelow", return_value=47):
            code, _ = issue_pairing_code()
            self.assertEqual(code, "0047")
            # Should consume without error
            consume_pairing_code("0047", "192.168.1.50")

    def test_invalid_length_and_non_numeric_codes_rejected(self):
        """Reject 3-digit, 5-digit, and non-numeric codes."""
        code, _ = issue_pairing_code()
        
        # 3 digits
        with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError):
            consume_pairing_code("123", "192.168.1.50")
        
        # 5 digits
        with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError):
            consume_pairing_code("12345", "192.168.1.50")
            
        # Non-numeric
        with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError):
            consume_pairing_code("abcd", "192.168.1.50")
        with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError):
            consume_pairing_code("12a4", "192.168.1.50")

    def test_code_is_single_use(self):
        """Verify single-use behavior."""
        code, expiry = issue_pairing_code()
        self.assertEqual(expiry, 300)
        consume_pairing_code(code, "192.168.1.20")
        with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError):
            consume_pairing_code(code, "192.168.1.20")

    def test_code_expiry(self):
        """Verify code expires after TTL."""
        import dispatch.sync.pairing_codes as pc
        code, _ = issue_pairing_code()
        # Simulate time passing past TTL
        with patch("time.monotonic", return_value=pc._active_code_expires_at + 1):
            with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError) as ctx:
                consume_pairing_code(code, "192.168.1.20")
            self.assertEqual(ctx.exception.status_code, 401)

    def test_lockout_protection_after_five_failed_attempts(self):
        """Verify IP lockout after 5 consecutive invalid attempts."""
        code, _ = issue_pairing_code()
        client_ip = "192.168.1.99"
        
        # 4 failed attempts
        for _ in range(4):
            with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError) as ctx:
                consume_pairing_code("0000" if code != "0000" else "9999", client_ip)
            self.assertEqual(ctx.exception.status_code, 401)
            
        # 5th failed attempt triggers lockout
        with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError) as ctx:
            consume_pairing_code("0000" if code != "0000" else "9999", client_ip)
        self.assertEqual(ctx.exception.status_code, 401)
        
        # Subsequent attempt (even with valid code!) is locked out with 429
        with self.assertRaises(PairingCodeError) as ctx:
            consume_pairing_code(code, client_ip)
        self.assertEqual(ctx.exception.status_code, 429)
        self.assertIn("Too many incorrect pairing attempts", str(ctx.exception))

    def test_complete_pc_generation_to_phone_pairing_flow(self):
        """REMOVED-FLOW CONTRACT (2026-10-24 pivot): PC->phone credential pairing no
        longer exists; on-device OAuth replaces it. Both routes fail closed with 410
        and NEVER return credential material under any input."""
        from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
        from dispatch.web.app import app
        client = TestClient(app)
        pc_resp = client.post(
            "/api/integrations/youtube/pairing-code",
            json={"understands_http_risk": True},
            headers={"host": "localhost:8000"}
        )
        self.assertEqual(pc_resp.status_code, 410)
        phone_resp = client.post(
            "/api/youtube/credentials",
            json={"pairing_code": "1234"},
        )
        self.assertEqual(phone_resp.status_code, 410)
        for body in (pc_resp.text, phone_resp.text):
            for marker in ("refresh_token", "client_secret", "mock-refresh-token"):
                self.assertNotIn(marker, body)


============================================================
FILE: tests\test_youtube_caption_timing_fix.py
============================================================
"""Acceptance Test Suite for YouTube Caption Timing & Karaoke Grouping.
Validates:
1. Word-timed VTT preserves real word boundaries through export.
2. Phrase-only VTT does not produce fabricated exact word timings.
3. Three-word caption grouping does not alter source timestamps.
4. Clip trimming does not introduce a constant timing offset.
5. Preview and rendered output stay synchronized.
6. Missing YouTube captions never trigger local Whisper.
7. Existing caption editing and Apply to All styling continue to work.
"""
import unittest
import tempfile
import json
from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import patch, MagicMock
from datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta

from dispatch.youtube_inbox.vtt_parser import parse_vtt_content
from dispatch.captions.model import CaptionTrack, CaptionGroup, Word, StyleConfig, LayoutConfig, AnimationConfig
from dispatch.captions.grouper import group_words_into_captions
from dispatch.captions.exporter import export_to_ass
from dispatch.transcription.worker import TranscriptionWorker
from dispatch.youtube_inbox.catcher import YouTubeInboxCatcher
from dispatch import db


class TestYouTubeCaptionTimingFix(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        # Configure test database in-memory or isolated
        pass

    def test_1_word_timed_vtt_preserves_real_word_boundaries_through_export(self):
        """1. Word-timed VTT preserves the real word boundaries through export."""
        vtt_content = """WEBVTT

00:00:01.000 --> 00:00:03.500
<00:00:01.000><c> Hello</c><00:00:01.500><c> world</c><00:00:02.000><c> welcome</c>
"""
        segments = parse_vtt_content(vtt_content)
        self.assertEqual(len(segments), 1)
        seg = segments[0]
        self.assertTrue(seg.get("has_word_timestamps"))
        self.assertEqual(len(seg["words"]), 3)

        # Check real boundaries from VTT tags
        w0 = seg["words"][0]
        w1 = seg["words"][1]
        w2 = seg["words"][2]

        self.assertEqual(w0["word"], "Hello")
        self.assertEqual(w0["start"], 1.000)
        self.assertEqual(w0["end"], 1.500)
        self.assertTrue(w0["is_exact"])

        self.assertEqual(w1["word"], "world")
        self.assertEqual(w1["start"], 1.500)
        self.assertEqual(w1["end"], 2.000)
        self.assertTrue(w1["is_exact"])

        self.assertEqual(w2["word"], "welcome")
        self.assertEqual(w2["start"], 2.000)
        self.assertEqual(w2["end"], 3.500)
        self.assertTrue(w2["is_exact"])

        # Group words into captions
        track = group_words_into_captions(segments, clip_start=0.0, clip_end=5.0)
        self.assertEqual(len(track.groups), 1)
        grp = track.groups[0]
        self.assertTrue(grp.has_word_timing)
        self.assertEqual(grp.timing_mode, "word")

        # Export to ASS
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
            ass_path = Path(tmpdir) / "test.ass"
            export_to_ass(track, ass_path)
            content = ass_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")

            # Dialogue events must reflect authentic word boundaries:
            # Word 0: 1.00s -> 1.50s (0:00:01.00 -> 0:00:01.50)
            self.assertIn("Dialogue: 0,0:00:01.00,0:00:01.50", content)
            # Word 1: 1.50s -> 2.00s (0:00:01.50 -> 0:00:02.00)
            self.assertIn("Dialogue: 0,0:00:01.50,0:00:02.00", content)
            # Word 2: 2.00s -> 3.50s (0:00:02.00 -> 0:00:03.50)
            self.assertIn("Dialogue: 0,0:00:02.00,0:00:03.50", content)

    def test_2_phrase_only_vtt_does_not_produce_fabricated_exact_word_timings(self):
        """2. Phrase-only VTT does not produce fabricated exact word timings."""
        vtt_content = """WEBVTT

00:00:01.000 --> 00:00:04.000
This is a standard YouTube manual caption.
"""
        segments = parse_vtt_content(vtt_content)
        self.assertEqual(len(segments), 1)
        seg = segments[0]
        # Must NOT be marked as having authentic word timestamps
        self.assertFalse(seg.get("has_word_timestamps"))

        # Words must have is_exact=False and NOT have divided progressive timestamps
        words = seg["words"]
        for w in words:
            self.assertFalse(w["is_exact"])
            # Duration must not be linearly divided (e.g. 1.0 -> 1.57, 1.57 -> 2.14)
            self.assertEqual(w["start"], 1.000)
            self.assertEqual(w["end"], 4.000)

        # Group words into captions
        track = group_words_into_captions(segments, clip_start=0.0, clip_end=5.0)
        self.assertEqual(len(track.groups), 1)
        grp = track.groups[0]
        self.assertFalse(grp.has_word_timing)
        self.assertEqual(grp.timing_mode, "phrase")

        # Export to ASS must render as single phrase dialogue without karaoke flashing
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
            ass_path = Path(tmpdir) / "phrase.ass"
            export_to_ass(track, ass_path)
            content = ass_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            dialogue_lines = [l for l in content.splitlines() if l.startswith("Dialogue:")]
            # Exactly 1 dialogue event for the phrase, not 7 events for each word
            self.assertEqual(len(dialogue_lines), 1)
            self.assertIn("0:00:01.00,0:00:04.00", dialogue_lines[0])
            # ASS uses \N as an explicit visual line break; normalize it when
            # asserting that phrase-mode text is preserved in one dialogue event.
            dialogue_text = dialogue_lines[0].replace(r"\N", " ")
            self.assertIn("THIS IS A STANDARD YOUTUBE MANUAL CAPTION.", dialogue_text)

    def test_3_three_word_caption_grouping_does_not_alter_source_timestamps(self):
        """3. Three-word caption grouping does not alter source timestamps."""
        raw_segments = [
            {
                "start": 1.0,
                "end": 2.1,
                "words": [
                    {"word": "One", "start": 1.000, "end": 1.300, "is_exact": True},
                    {"word": "Two", "start": 1.400, "end": 1.700, "is_exact": True},
                    {"word": "Three", "start": 1.800, "end": 2.100, "is_exact": True},
                ]
            },
            {
                "start": 2.25,
                "end": 3.0,
                "words": [
                    {"word": "Next", "start": 2.250, "end": 2.800, "is_exact": True}
                ]
            }
        ]

        # Group into captions (gap between 2.100 and 2.250 is 0.15s < 0.25s, so card end will stretch)
        track = group_words_into_captions(raw_segments, clip_start=0.0, clip_end=5.0, words_per_group=3)
        self.assertEqual(len(track.groups), 2)

        grp0 = track.groups[0]
        # Card end was stretched to 2.25 to prevent visual gap flicker
        self.assertEqual(grp0.end, 2.250)

        # BUT the 3rd word's authentic source timestamp MUST remain untouched!
        w_last = grp0.words[2]
        self.assertEqual(w_last.text, "Three")
        self.assertEqual(w_last.start, 1.800)
        self.assertEqual(w_last.end, 2.100, "Source word timestamp must NOT be mutated by gap closure!")

    def test_4_clip_trimming_does_not_introduce_constant_timing_offset(self):
        """4. Clip trimming does not introduce a constant timing offset."""
        # Absolute source video timestamps: 45.0s to 50.0s
        raw_segments = [
            {
                "start": 45.0,
                "end": 47.0,
                "words": [
                    {"word": "Highlight", "start": 45.200, "end": 45.800, "is_exact": True},
                    {"word": "moment", "start": 46.000, "end": 46.700, "is_exact": True}
                ]
            }
        ]

        # Clip trimmed with clip_start=40.0s
        track = group_words_into_captions(raw_segments, clip_start=40.0, clip_end=55.0)
        self.assertEqual(len(track.groups), 1)
        w0 = track.groups[0].words[0]
        w1 = track.groups[0].words[1]

        # Converted to clip-relative time exactly once: 45.2 - 40.0 = 5.2s
        self.assertAlmostEqual(w0.start, 5.200, places=3)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(w0.end, 5.800, places=3)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(w1.start, 6.000, places=3)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(w1.end, 6.700, places=3)

        # Check rolling cue deduplication does not introduce constant offset
        rolling_vtt = """WEBVTT

00:00:01.000 --> 00:00:03.000
First line

00:00:02.000 --> 00:00:04.500
First line second line
"""
        deduped = parse_vtt_content(rolling_vtt)
        self.assertEqual(len(deduped), 2)
        # First cue ends at 3.000s
        self.assertEqual(deduped[0]["end"], 3.000)
        # Second deduped cue "second line" must start at 3.000s, NOT at 2.000s (which would be 1.0s too early!)
        self.assertEqual(deduped[1]["text"], "second line")
        self.assertEqual(deduped[1]["start"], 3.000)

    def test_5_preview_and_rendered_output_stay_synchronized(self):
        """5. Preview and rendered output stay synchronized."""
        raw_segments = [
            {
                "start": 10.0,
                "end": 12.0,
                "words": [
                    {"word": "Sync", "start": 10.200, "end": 10.600, "is_exact": True},
                    {"word": "test", "start": 10.700, "end": 11.200, "is_exact": True}
                ]
            }
        ]
        track = group_words_into_captions(raw_segments, clip_start=10.0, clip_end=15.0)
        w0 = track.groups[0].words[0]
        w1 = track.groups[0].words[1]

        # Frontend preview uses currentTime >= w.start && currentTime <= w.end
        # Preview active window for w0: [0.200, 0.600]
        # Preview active window for w1: [0.700, 1.200]
        self.assertEqual(w0.start, 0.200)
        self.assertEqual(w0.end, 0.600)
        self.assertEqual(w1.start, 0.700)
        self.assertEqual(w1.end, 1.200)

        # Exported ASS must have exact same timecodes
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
            ass_path = Path(tmpdir) / "sync.ass"
            export_to_ass(track, ass_path)
            content = ass_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")

            # Dialogue for w0 active illumination: 0:00:00.20 to 0:00:00.60
            self.assertIn("Dialogue: 0,0:00:00.20,0:00:00.60", content)
            # Dialogue for w1 active illumination: 0:00:00.70 to 0:00:01.20
            self.assertIn("Dialogue: 0,0:00:00.70,0:00:01.20", content)

    def test_6_missing_youtube_captions_never_trigger_local_whisper(self):
        """6. Missing YouTube captions never trigger local Whisper."""
        video_id = "test_no_whisper_yt"
        db.register_youtube_video(video_id=video_id, title="No Whisper Video", duration=120.0)
        past_deadline = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(minutes=10)).strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
        db.update_youtube_video(
            video_id=video_id,
            status="WAITING_FOR_TRANSCRIPT",
            transcript_wait_deadline=past_deadline
        )

        mock_catcher = MagicMock()
        mock_catcher.fetch_youtube_captions.return_value = None

        with patch("dispatch.transcription.worker.transcribe_video") as mock_whisper:
            worker = TranscriptionWorker(caption_wait_seconds=60, catcher=mock_catcher)
            res = worker.process_pending()

            # Whisper must NEVER be invoked!
            mock_whisper.assert_not_called()

            # Result must be CAPTIONS_UNAVAILABLE
            self.assertEqual(len(res), 1)
            self.assertEqual(res[0]["status"], "CAPTIONS_UNAVAILABLE")

        # Database record must reflect CAPTIONS_UNAVAILABLE
        yt_rec = db.get_youtube_video(video_id)
        self.assertEqual(yt_rec["status"], "CAPTIONS_UNAVAILABLE")
        self.assertIn("unavailable", yt_rec["last_error"].lower())

    def test_7_existing_caption_editing_and_apply_to_all_styling_continue_to_work(self):
        """7. Existing caption editing and Apply to All styling continue to work."""
        # Build multi-group track
        g1 = CaptionGroup(
            id="grp_1",
            start=0.0,
            end=2.0,
            words=[Word(id="w1", text="First", start=0.0, end=1.0), Word(id="w2", text="card", start=1.0, end=2.0)],
            style=StyleConfig(font_family="Impact", font_size=54, primary_color="#FFFFFF")
        )
        g2 = CaptionGroup(
            id="grp_2",
            start=2.5,
            end=4.5,
            words=[Word(id="w3", text="Second", start=2.5, end=3.5), Word(id="w4", text="card", start=3.5, end=4.5)],
            style=StyleConfig(font_family="Roboto", font_size=42, primary_color="#00FF00")
        )
        track = CaptionTrack(clip_id="clip_apply_all", groups=[g1, g2])

        # Test JSON round-trip
        data = track.to_dict()
        reconstituted = CaptionTrack.from_dict(data)

        self.assertEqual(len(reconstituted.groups), 2)
        self.assertEqual(reconstituted.groups[0].style.font_family, "Impact")
        self.assertEqual(reconstituted.groups[1].style.font_family, "Roboto")
        self.assertTrue(reconstituted.groups[0].has_word_timing)

        # Simulate editing caption text
        reconstituted.groups[0].words[0].text = "EditedFirst"
        self.assertEqual(reconstituted.groups[0].words[0].text, "EditedFirst")

        # Export to ASS works with custom styles
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
            ass_path = Path(tmpdir) / "edited.ass"
            export_to_ass(reconstituted, ass_path)
            content = ass_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            self.assertIn("EDITEDFIRST", content)
            self.assertIn("SECOND", content)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

"""Unit and Integration Tests for YouTube Cloud Inbox & Catcher Architecture.
Verifies WebVTT subtitle parsing, database idempotency, video ID extraction,
and end-to-end pipeline execution with YouTube captions and Whisper fallback.
"""
import unittest
import tempfile
import json
from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import patch, MagicMock

from dispatch.youtube_inbox.vtt_parser import (
    parse_timestamp_to_seconds,
    clean_vtt_text,
    parse_vtt_content,
    parse_vtt_file
)
from dispatch.youtube_inbox.catcher import (
    extract_youtube_video_id,
    YouTubeInboxCatcher
)
from dispatch import db

# Privacy-gate fixture: ingestion requires an allowed visibility verdict.
patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status",
      side_effect=lambda ids: {i: {"privacy_status": "unlisted"} for i in ids}).start()


class TestVTTParser(unittest.TestCase):
    """Test WebVTT subtitle parsing and normalization."""

    def test_parse_timestamp_to_seconds(self):
        self.assertEqual(parse_timestamp_to_seconds("00", "01", "23", "456"), 83.456)
        self.assertEqual(parse_timestamp_to_seconds(None, "02", "15", "500"), 135.5)
        self.assertEqual(parse_timestamp_to_seconds("01", "00", "00", "000"), 3600.0)

    def test_clean_vtt_text(self):
        raw = "<c>Hello</c> &amp; <00:00:01.200>welcome align:start position:50%"
        cleaned = clean_vtt_text(raw)
        self.assertEqual(cleaned, "Hello & welcome")

    def test_parse_vtt_content_deduplication_and_merging(self):
        sample_vtt = """WEBVTT
Kind: captions
Language: en

00:00:01.000 --> 00:00:03.000
Here is the biggest mistake

00:00:03.100 --> 00:00:05.500
Here is the biggest mistake founders make

00:00:06.000 --> 00:00:09.000
They build before talking to users.
"""
        segments = parse_vtt_content(sample_vtt)
        self.assertGreater(len(segments), 0)

        # Ensure deduplicated: "founders make" should not repeat the whole prefix
        full_text = " ".join(s["text"] for s in segments)
        self.assertIn("biggest mistake", full_text)
        self.assertIn("founders make", full_text)
        self.assertIn("talking to users", full_text)

        # Check word-level timestamps generated
        for seg in segments:
            self.assertIn("start", seg)
            self.assertIn("end", seg)
            self.assertIn("words", seg)
            self.assertGreater(len(seg["words"]), 0)


class TestYouTubeInboxDatabase(unittest.TestCase):
    """Test SQLite database operations and idempotency for YouTube inbox."""

    def setUp(self):
        db.init_db()
        with db.get_db_connection() as conn:
            conn.execute("DELETE FROM youtube_inbox WHERE video_id LIKE 'test_vid_%'")
            conn.execute("DELETE FROM youtube_inbox WHERE video_id LIKE 'mock_vid_%'")
            conn.commit()

    def test_idempotent_registration(self):
        test_id = "test_vid_001"
        # First registration should succeed
        is_new = db.register_youtube_video(
            video_id=test_id,
            title="[DISPATCH] Test Video 1",
            channel_id="UC12345",
            upload_time="2026-10-06",
            duration=45.0
        )
        self.assertTrue(is_new)

        # Duplicate registration should return False (idempotent)
        is_dup = db.register_youtube_video(
            video_id=test_id,
            title="[DISPATCH] Test Video 1 Duplicate",
            channel_id="UC12345"
        )
        self.assertFalse(is_dup)

        # Fetch and verify fields
        record = db.get_youtube_video(test_id)
        self.assertIsNotNone(record)
        self.assertEqual(record["title"], "[DISPATCH] Test Video 1")
        self.assertEqual(record["status"], "DISCOVERED")

        # Update status and check
        db.update_youtube_video(test_id, status="CLIPS_CREATED", transcript_source="youtube")
        updated = db.get_youtube_video(test_id)
        self.assertEqual(updated["status"], "CLIPS_CREATED")
        self.assertEqual(updated["transcript_source"], "youtube")
        self.assertTrue(db.is_youtube_video_processed(test_id))


class TestYouTubeCatcher(unittest.TestCase):
    """Test video ID parsing, catcher methods, and mock end-to-end execution."""

    def setUp(self):
        db.init_db()
        with db.get_db_connection() as conn:
            conn.execute("DELETE FROM youtube_inbox WHERE video_id LIKE 'mock_vid_%'")
            conn.commit()

    def test_extract_youtube_video_id(self):
        # Full URL
        self.assertEqual(
            extract_youtube_video_id("https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dQw4w9WgXcQ"),
            "dQw4w9WgXcQ"
        )
        # Short URL
        self.assertEqual(
            extract_youtube_video_id("https://youtu.be/dQw4w9WgXcQ"),
            "dQw4w9WgXcQ"
        )
        # Shorts URL
        self.assertEqual(
            extract_youtube_video_id("https://www.youtube.com/shorts/dQw4w9WgXcQ"),
            "dQw4w9WgXcQ"
        )
        # Raw 11-char ID
        self.assertEqual(
            extract_youtube_video_id("dQw4w9WgXcQ"),
            "dQw4w9WgXcQ"
        )
        # Invalid URL
        self.assertIsNone(extract_youtube_video_id("invalid-link"))

    @patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.catcher.identify_and_save_highlights")
    @patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.catcher.render_clip")
    def test_process_video_mock_pipeline(self, mock_render, mock_highlights):
        catcher = YouTubeInboxCatcher()
        test_id = "mock_vid_99"

        # Mock dependencies
        catcher.fetch_video_info = MagicMock(return_value={
            "id": test_id,
            "title": "[DISPATCH] 2026-10-06 14:30 Work Session",
            "channel_id": "UC_TEST",
            "channel": "Test Channel",
            "duration": 40.0,
            "upload_date": "2026-10-06",
            "description": "Autonomous Dispatch test"
        })

        # Mock download returning a dummy file
        with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix=".mp4", delete=False) as f:
            dummy_video_path = Path(f.name)
        catcher.download_video = MagicMock(return_value=dummy_video_path)

        # Mock captions returning structured segments
        mock_segments = [
            {"start": 0.0, "end": 15.0, "text": "Basically here is the trick to automate video production.", "words": []},
            {"start": 15.0, "end": 35.0, "text": "You record on your phone, upload to YouTube private, and laptop catches it.", "words": []}
        ]
        catcher.fetch_youtube_captions = MagicMock(return_value=mock_segments)

        # Mock highlight finder to register real clip in database
        def fake_identify(chunk_id, session_id, segments, total_duration, publish_mode="private"):
            cid = db.save_clip(
                session_id=session_id,
                chunk_id=chunk_id,
                start_time=0.0,
                end_time=15.0,
                title="Mock Clip",
                hook="Hook",
                description="Desc",
                hashtags="#Shorts",
                virality_score=80,
                layout_mode="fit_blur"
            )
            return [cid]

        mock_highlights.side_effect = fake_identify

        # Mock renderer
        mock_render.return_value = {
            "video_path": dummy_video_path,
            "thumbnail_path": dummy_video_path.with_suffix(".jpg"),
            "duration": 20.0
        }

        # Run process_video
        result = catcher.process_video(f"https://youtu.be/{test_id}")

        self.assertEqual(result["video_id"], test_id)
        self.assertEqual(result["status"], "CLIPS_CREATED")
        self.assertEqual(result["transcript_source"], "youtube")
        self.assertEqual(result["clips_count"], 1)

        # Verify database record updated
        rec = db.get_youtube_video(test_id)
        self.assertIsNotNone(rec)
        self.assertEqual(rec["status"], "CLIPS_CREATED")

        # Verify idempotency on second call
        second_result = catcher.process_video(f"https://youtu.be/{test_id}")
        self.assertEqual(second_result["status"], "ALREADY_PROCESSED")

        # Cleanup
        try:
            dummy_video_path.unlink()
        except Exception:
            pass


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()


============================================================
FILE: tests\test_youtube_ingest_endpoint.py
============================================================
"""Route B regression tests: manual YouTube link ingestion via the real dashboard endpoint.

Covers:
  1. Invalid URL -> HTTP 400 and no ingestion started.
  2. Valid submission -> HTTP 200 processing_queued and the real ingestion runs with the submitted URL.
  3. Concurrent duplicate submission -> exactly one ingestion run (real single-flight guard,
     exercised at both the catcher level and through the endpoint).
  4. Download failure -> FAILED status with last_error visible via GET /api/youtube/inbox.
  5. Re-submission of a video already processed by downstream workers (COMPLETED) -> no re-download.

Only the network boundary (yt-dlp metadata/download, YouTube Data API status) is mocked;
the endpoint, background threading, single-flight guard, DB state machine, and inbox API are real.
"""
import shutil
import tempfile
import threading
import time
import unittest
from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import patch

from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

from dispatch import db
from dispatch.web.app import app
from dispatch.youtube_inbox.catcher import (
    YouTubeInboxCatcher,
    _INGEST_INFLIGHT,
    is_ingestion_in_flight,
)

client = TestClient(app)


class TestYouTubeIngestEndpoint(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        db.init_db()
        self.temp_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())

    def tearDown(self):
        shutil.rmtree(self.temp_dir, ignore_errors=True)
        with db.get_db_connection() as conn:
            conn.execute("DELETE FROM youtube_inbox WHERE video_id LIKE 'tt_ep%'")
        with _INGEST_INFLIGHT_LOCK_SAFE():
            _INGEST_INFLIGHT.clear()

    def test_01_invalid_url_rejected_with_400(self):
        """1. An invalid URL is rejected with HTTP 400 and no ingestion is started."""
        with patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "ingest_video") as mock_ingest:
            res = client.post("/api/youtube/ingest", json={"url": "definitely not a youtube link"})
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 400)
        self.assertIn("Invalid", res.json()["detail"])
        mock_ingest.assert_not_called()

    def test_02_valid_url_accepted_and_ingestion_started(self):
        """2. A valid submission returns processing_queued and starts the real ingestion thread."""
        vid = "tt_ep_20001"
        started = threading.Event()
        release = threading.Event()
        captured = {}

        def fake_ingest(self, url_or_id, **kwargs):
            captured["url"] = url_or_id
            started.set()
            release.wait(timeout=5)
            return {"video_id": vid, "status": "DOWNLOADED", "verified": True}

        with patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "ingest_video", fake_ingest):
            res = client.post("/api/youtube/ingest", json={"url": f"https://youtu.be/{vid}"})

        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
        data = res.json()
        self.assertEqual(data["status"], "processing_queued")
        self.assertEqual(data["video_id"], vid)

        self.assertTrue(started.wait(timeout=5), "Background ingestion thread did not start")
        self.assertEqual(captured["url"], f"https://youtu.be/{vid}")
        release.set()

    def test_03_catcher_guard_allows_only_one_concurrent_run(self):
        """3a. Two concurrent ingest_video calls for the same video: the second is refused
        by the real single-flight guard and only one download runs."""
        vid = "tt_ep_30001"
        entered_fetch = threading.Event()
        release_fetch = threading.Event()
        download_calls = []

        def fake_fetch_info(self, video_id):
            entered_fetch.set()
            release_fetch.wait(timeout=5)
            return {"id": vid, "title": "Guard Test", "channel_id": "", "channel": "",
                    "duration": 10.0, "upload_date": "", "description": ""}

        def fake_download(self, video_id):
            download_calls.append(video_id)
            f = self.storage_dir / f"{video_id}.mp4"
            f.write_bytes(b"x" * 2048)
            return f

        catcher = YouTubeInboxCatcher(storage_dir=self.temp_dir)
        results = {}

        def run_once(key):
            results[key] = catcher.ingest_video(vid)

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status",
                       side_effect=lambda ids: {i: {"privacy_status": "unlisted"} for i in ids}), \
             patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "fetch_video_info", fake_fetch_info), \
             patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "download_video", fake_download), \
             patch("dispatch.ingestion.validator.probe_video",
                   return_value=(True, {"duration": 10.0, "width": 1080, "height": 1920}, None)):

            t1 = threading.Thread(target=run_once, args=("first",))
            t1.start()
            self.assertTrue(entered_fetch.wait(timeout=5), "First ingestion never reached metadata fetch")
            self.assertTrue(is_ingestion_in_flight(vid), "In-flight flag must be set during a run")

            t2 = threading.Thread(target=run_once, args=("second",))
            t2.start()
            t2.join(timeout=5)
            self.assertFalse(t2.is_alive())

            self.assertEqual(results["second"]["status"], "ALREADY_INGESTING")
            self.assertTrue(results["second"].get("duplicate"))

            release_fetch.set()
            t1.join(timeout=5)

            self.assertEqual(results["first"]["status"], "DOWNLOADED")
            self.assertEqual(download_calls, [vid], "Exactly one download run must occur")

        self.assertFalse(is_ingestion_in_flight(vid), "Guard slot must be released in finally after completion")

    def test_04_endpoint_reports_already_processing_for_in_flight_video(self):
        """3b. A repeat POST while a real ingestion is in flight returns a distinct
        already_processing status, not a second processing_queued run."""
        vid = "tt_ep_40001"
        entered_fetch = threading.Event()
        release_fetch = threading.Event()

        def fake_fetch_info(self, video_id):
            entered_fetch.set()
            release_fetch.wait(timeout=5)
            return {"id": vid, "title": "Endpoint Guard Test", "channel_id": "", "channel": "",
                    "duration": 10.0, "upload_date": "", "description": ""}

        def fake_download(self, video_id):
            f = self.storage_dir / f"{video_id}.mp4"
            f.write_bytes(b"x" * 2048)
            return f

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status",
                       side_effect=lambda ids: {i: {"privacy_status": "unlisted"} for i in ids}), \
             patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "fetch_video_info", fake_fetch_info), \
             patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "download_video", fake_download), \
             patch("dispatch.ingestion.validator.probe_video",
                   return_value=(True, {"duration": 10.0, "width": 1080, "height": 1920}, None)):

            first = client.post("/api/youtube/ingest", json={"url": f"https://youtu.be/{vid}"})
            self.assertEqual(first.status_code, 200)
            self.assertEqual(first.json()["status"], "processing_queued")

            self.assertTrue(entered_fetch.wait(timeout=5), "First ingestion never started")

            second = client.post("/api/youtube/ingest", json={"url": f"https://youtu.be/{vid}"})
            self.assertEqual(second.status_code, 200)
            body = second.json()
            self.assertEqual(body["status"], "already_processing")
            self.assertTrue(body.get("duplicate"))
            self.assertEqual(body["video_id"], vid)

            release_fetch.set()

            # Let the first ingestion thread finish inside this patch context so it
            # cannot leak its download call into a later test's mocks.
            deadline = time.time() + 5
            while time.time() < deadline and is_ingestion_in_flight(vid):
                time.sleep(0.05)
            self.assertFalse(is_ingestion_in_flight(vid), "First ingestion thread did not finish")

    def test_05_download_failure_recorded_and_visible_in_inbox(self):
        """4. A failed download leaves a visible FAILED record with last_error via the inbox API."""
        vid = "tt_ep_50001"
        done = threading.Event()

        def fake_fetch_info(self, video_id):
            raise RuntimeError("unavailable")

        def failing_download(self, video_id):
            raise RuntimeError("ERROR: requested format not available")

        real_ingest = YouTubeInboxCatcher.ingest_video

        def wrapped_ingest(self, url_or_id, **kwargs):
            try:
                return real_ingest(self, url_or_id, **kwargs)
            finally:
                done.set()

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status",
                       side_effect=lambda ids: {i: {"privacy_status": "unlisted"} for i in ids}), \
             patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "fetch_video_info", fake_fetch_info), \
             patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "download_video", failing_download), \
             patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "ingest_video", wrapped_ingest):

            res = client.post("/api/youtube/ingest", json={"url": f"https://youtu.be/{vid}"})
            self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
            self.assertEqual(res.json()["status"], "processing_queued")

            self.assertTrue(done.wait(timeout=10), "Background ingestion did not finish")

        res = client.get("/api/youtube/inbox")
        self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
        items = {i["video_id"]: i for i in res.json()["items"]}
        self.assertIn(vid, items)
        record = items[vid]
        self.assertEqual(record["status"], "FAILED")
        self.assertIn("Download failed", record["last_error"])
        self.assertIn("requested format not available", record["last_error"])

    def test_06_resubmitting_downstream_completed_video_does_not_redownload(self):
        """5. A video already fully processed by downstream workers (COMPLETED) with a valid
        local file is not downloaded again on re-import."""
        vid = "tt_ep_60001"
        fake_file = self.temp_dir / f"{vid}.mp4"
        fake_file.write_bytes(b"v" * 2048)
        db.register_youtube_video(video_id=vid, title="Downstream Completed")
        db.update_youtube_video(vid, status="COMPLETED", local_video_path=str(fake_file))

        with patch.object(YouTubeInboxCatcher, "download_video") as mock_download, \
             patch("dispatch.ingestion.validator.probe_video",
                   return_value=(True, {"duration": 10.0, "width": 1080, "height": 1920}, None)):
            res = client.post("/api/youtube/ingest", json={"url": f"https://youtu.be/{vid}"})
            self.assertEqual(res.status_code, 200)
            self.assertEqual(res.json()["video_id"], vid)
            # Give the background thread time to run the real ingest_video path.
            deadline = time.time() + 2.0
            while time.time() < deadline and not mock_download.called:
                time.sleep(0.05)
            time.sleep(0.3)
            mock_download.assert_not_called()

        record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
        self.assertEqual(record["status"], "COMPLETED")


def _INGEST_INFLIGHT_LOCK_SAFE():
    from dispatch.youtube_inbox.catcher import _INGEST_INFLIGHT_LOCK
    return _INGEST_INFLIGHT_LOCK


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()


============================================================
FILE: tests\test_youtube_processing_tracker.py
============================================================
"""Focused tests for YouTube Processing Status Tracking, Deferred Ingestion, and Stall Detection.
Verifies:
  1. A video reported as processing is deferred without repeated media downloads.
  2. A video whose processing later succeeds is automatically ingested.
  3. A permanent processing failure is recorded with useful diagnostic information.
  4. Transient API or download errors are handled without corrupting video state.
  5. A video waiting for processing is surfaced as stalled when exceeding timeout without false completion.
  6. Batch status query handles multiple video IDs accurately.
"""
import unittest
import tempfile
import shutil
import os
from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch

from dispatch import db
from dispatch.youtube_inbox.catcher import YouTubeInboxCatcher
from dispatch.youtube_inbox.poller import YouTubeInboxPoller
from dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth import get_youtube_processing_status


class TestYouTubeProcessingTracker(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        db.init_db()
        self.temp_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())
        self.catcher = YouTubeInboxCatcher(storage_dir=self.temp_dir)
        self.poller = YouTubeInboxPoller(catcher=self.catcher)

    def tearDown(self):
        shutil.rmtree(self.temp_dir, ignore_errors=True)
        with db.get_db_connection() as conn:
            conn.execute("DELETE FROM youtube_inbox WHERE video_id LIKE 'tt_%'")

    def test_01_processing_video_is_deferred_without_download(self):
        """1. A video reported as processing is deferred without calling download_video."""
        vid = "tt_proc0001"
        mock_status = {
            vid: {
                "video_id": vid,
                "privacy_status": "unlisted",
                "upload_status": "uploaded",
                "processing_status": "processing",
                "failure_reason": None,
                "duration": "P0D",
                "title": "[DISPATCH] Processing Test",
                "published_at": "2026-10-09T18:00:00Z"
            }
        }

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", return_value=mock_status), \
             patch.object(self.catcher, "download_video") as mock_download:

            result = self.catcher.ingest_video(url_or_id=vid)

            # Assert download was NEVER called
            mock_download.assert_not_called()

            # Assert returned deferred status
            self.assertEqual(result["status"], "WAITING_FOR_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING")
            self.assertTrue(result["processing"])
            self.assertFalse(result["verified"])

            # Assert database state
            record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
            self.assertIsNotNone(record)
            self.assertEqual(record["status"], "WAITING_FOR_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING")

    def test_02_processing_succeeded_advances_to_download_and_ingestion(self):
        """2. A video whose processing succeeds proceeds to download and completes ingestion."""
        vid = "tt_succ0002"
        mock_status = {
            vid: {
                "video_id": vid,
                "privacy_status": "unlisted",
                "upload_status": "processed",
                "processing_status": "succeeded",
                "failure_reason": None,
                "duration": "PT45S",
                "title": "[DISPATCH] Succeeded Test",
                "published_at": "2026-10-09T18:00:00Z"
            }
        }

        fake_video_file = self.temp_dir / f"{vid}.mp4"
        fake_video_file.write_bytes(b"dummy_content")

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", return_value=mock_status), \
             patch.object(self.catcher, "download_video", return_value=fake_video_file) as mock_download, \
             patch("dispatch.ingestion.validator.probe_video", return_value=(True, {"duration": 45.0, "width": 1080, "height": 1920}, None)):

            result = self.catcher.ingest_video(url_or_id=vid)

            mock_download.assert_called_once_with(vid)
            self.assertEqual(result["status"], "DOWNLOADED")
            self.assertTrue(result["verified"])

            record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
            self.assertEqual(record["status"], "DOWNLOADED")
            self.assertEqual(record["duration"], 45.0)

    def test_03_processing_failed_records_diagnostic_information(self):
        """3. A permanent processing failure records failure reason and transitions to FAILED."""
        vid = "tt_fail0003"
        mock_status = {
            vid: {
                "video_id": vid,
                "privacy_status": "unlisted",
                "upload_status": "failed",
                "processing_status": "failed",
                "failure_reason": "transcodingFailed",
                "duration": "P0D",
                "title": "[DISPATCH] Failed Video",
                "published_at": "2026-10-09T18:00:00Z"
            }
        }

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", return_value=mock_status), \
             patch.object(self.catcher, "download_video") as mock_download:

            result = self.catcher.ingest_video(url_or_id=vid)

            mock_download.assert_not_called()
            self.assertEqual(result["status"], "FAILED")
            self.assertIn("transcodingFailed", result["error"])

            record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
            self.assertEqual(record["status"], "FAILED")
            self.assertIn("transcodingFailed", record["last_error"])

    def test_04_poller_waiting_video_staleness_detection(self):
        """4. Poller marks video stalled when elapsed processing time exceeds threshold without false completion."""
        vid = "tt_stal0004"
        # 2 hours before utcnow
        old_pub_time = "2026-10-09T18:00:00Z"

        db.register_youtube_video(
            video_id=vid,
            title="[DISPATCH] Stalled Video",
            upload_time=old_pub_time,
            dispatch_id="dsp_test_stall"
        )
        db.update_youtube_video(vid, status="WAITING_FOR_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING")

        mock_auth_uploads = [
            {
                "video_id": vid,
                "privacy_status": "unlisted",
                "title": "[DISPATCH] Stalled Video",
                "published_at": old_pub_time,
                "dispatch_id": "dsp_test_stall"
            }
        ]

        mock_status = {
            vid: {
                "video_id": vid,
                "privacy_status": "unlisted",
                "upload_status": "uploaded",
                "processing_status": "processing",
                "failure_reason": None,
                "duration": "P0D",
                "title": "[DISPATCH] Stalled Video",
                "published_at": old_pub_time
            }
        }

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.poller.list_authenticated_user_uploads", return_value=mock_auth_uploads), \
             patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", return_value=mock_status), \
             patch.object(self.catcher, "ingest_video") as mock_ingest:

            # Set timeout threshold to 60 minutes
            with patch.dict(os.environ, {"DISPATCH_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING_TIMEOUT_MINUTES": "60"}):
                self.poller.poll_once()

            # Assert ingest / download was NOT called
            mock_ingest.assert_not_called()

            # Mark it actionable without treating YouTube's delay as a permanent failure.
            record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
            self.assertEqual(record["status"], "STALLED_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING")
            self.assertIn("[STALLED_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING]", record["last_error"])

            # A later successful YouTube result must revisit this same video ID and resume ingestion.
            mock_status[vid]["processing_status"] = "succeeded"
            mock_status[vid]["upload_status"] = "processed"
            self.poller.poll_once()
            mock_ingest.assert_called_once_with(
                url_or_id=vid,
                dispatch_id="dsp_test_stall",
                session_id=None,
                sequence_number=None
            )

    def test_05_batch_status_query_parses_responses_correctly(self):
        """5. get_youtube_processing_status parses multiple items from service."""
        mock_service = MagicMock()
        mock_videos = MagicMock()
        mock_service.videos.return_value = mock_videos

        mock_resp = {
            "items": [
                {
                    "id": "vid_1",
                    "status": {"uploadStatus": "uploaded", "privacyStatus": "unlisted"},
                    "processingDetails": {"processingStatus": "processing"},
                    "contentDetails": {"duration": "P0D"},
                    "snippet": {"title": "Vid 1", "publishedAt": "2026-10-10T01:00:00Z"}
                },
                {
                    "id": "vid_2",
                    "status": {"uploadStatus": "processed", "privacyStatus": "unlisted"},
                    "processingDetails": {"processingStatus": "succeeded"},
                    "contentDetails": {"duration": "PT1M"},
                    "snippet": {"title": "Vid 2", "publishedAt": "2026-10-10T01:05:00Z"}
                }
            ]
        }
        mock_videos.list.return_value.execute.return_value = mock_resp

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_service", return_value=mock_service):
            res = get_youtube_processing_status(["vid_1", "vid_2"])

            self.assertEqual(len(res), 2)
            self.assertEqual(res["vid_1"]["processing_status"], "processing")
            self.assertEqual(res["vid_1"]["upload_status"], "uploaded")
            self.assertEqual(res["vid_2"]["processing_status"], "succeeded")
            self.assertEqual(res["vid_2"]["duration"], "PT1M")

    def test_06_transient_api_error_does_not_corrupt_state(self):
        """6. Transient visibility-API errors DEFER without downloading (fail-closed),
        keep a durable non-terminal record, and retry succeeds once the API recovers."""
        vid = "tt_tran0006"
        downloads = []
        def fake_download(catcher_self, v):
            downloads.append(v)
            raise Exception("download must not be reached on defer")
        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", side_effect=RuntimeError("Network timeout")),              patch.object(self.catcher, "download_video", fake_download):

            result = self.catcher.ingest_video(url_or_id=vid)

            self.assertEqual(result["status"], "WAITING_FOR_VISIBILITY_CHECK")
            self.assertEqual(downloads, [])  # invariant: no download before verified visibility
            record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
            self.assertIsNotNone(record)
            self.assertEqual(record["status"], "WAITING_FOR_VISIBILITY_CHECK")
            self.assertIn("deferred", record["last_error"] or "")

        # Retry once the visibility service recovers: the SAME video proceeds (not
        # permanently poisoned, not marked private/failed).
        import tempfile as _tf, shutil as _sh
        from pathlib import Path as _P
        tmp = _P(_tf.mkdtemp(prefix="t06_retry_"))
        (tmp / f"{vid}.mp4").write_bytes(b"x")
        try:
            with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status",
                       side_effect=lambda ids: {i: {"privacy_status": "unlisted"} for i in ids}),                  patch("dispatch.ingestion.validator.probe_video",
                       lambda f: (True, {"duration": 10.0, "width": 720, "height": 1280}, None)),                  patch.object(self.catcher, "fetch_video_info",
                              lambda self_, v: {"id": v, "title": "t", "duration": 10}),                  patch.object(self.catcher, "download_video", lambda self_, v: tmp / f"{v}.mp4"):
                retried = self.catcher.ingest_video(url_or_id=vid)
            self.assertNotEqual(retried["status"], "WAITING_FOR_VISIBILITY_CHECK")
            self.assertNotEqual(retried["status"], "FAILED")
        finally:
            _sh.rmtree(tmp, ignore_errors=True)


    def test_07_already_downloaded_video_skipped_idempotently(self):
        """7. An already-downloaded valid video returns immediately without re-downloading."""
        vid = "tt_done0007"
        fake_file = self.temp_dir / f"{vid}.mp4"
        fake_file.write_bytes(b"existing_video_bytes")

        db.register_youtube_video(video_id=vid, title="Already Downloaded")
        db.update_youtube_video(vid, status="DOWNLOADED", local_video_path=str(fake_file))

        with patch.object(self.catcher, "download_video") as mock_download, \
             patch("dispatch.ingestion.validator.probe_video", return_value=(True, {"duration": 30.0}, None)):

            result = self.catcher.ingest_video(url_or_id=vid)

            mock_download.assert_not_called()
            self.assertEqual(result["status"], "DOWNLOADED")
            self.assertTrue(result["verified"])

    def test_08_terminal_failure_wins_over_uploaded_status(self):
        """A failed processing state must not be mislabeled as still processing."""
        vid = "tt_failupl08"
        db.register_youtube_video(video_id=vid, title="Failed upload-status fixture")
        db.update_youtube_video(vid, status="WAITING_FOR_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING")
        status = {
            vid: {
                "video_id": vid,
                "privacy_status": "unlisted",
                "upload_status": "uploaded",
                "processing_status": "failed",
                "failure_reason": "transcodingFailed",
                "duration": "P0D",
            }
        }

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.poller.list_authenticated_user_uploads", return_value=[{"video_id": vid}]), \
             patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", return_value=status), \
             patch.object(self.catcher, "ingest_video") as mock_ingest:
            self.poller.poll_once()

        mock_ingest.assert_not_called()
        record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
        self.assertEqual(record["status"], "FAILED")
        self.assertIn("transcodingFailed", record["last_error"])
        self.assertIn("upload_status=uploaded", record["last_error"])

        terminal_error = record["last_error"]
        status[vid]["processing_status"] = None
        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.poller.list_authenticated_user_uploads", return_value=[{"video_id": vid}]), \
             patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", return_value=status), \
             patch.object(self.catcher, "ingest_video") as mock_ingest:
            self.poller.poll_once()
        mock_ingest.assert_not_called()
        record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
        self.assertEqual(record["status"], "FAILED")
        self.assertEqual(record["last_error"], terminal_error)

    def test_09_status_api_error_preserves_existing_state_and_does_not_download(self):
        """A status API outage is visible and cannot trigger an unverified media download."""
        vid = "tt_apierr09"
        db.register_youtube_video(video_id=vid, title="Status API outage fixture")
        db.update_youtube_video(vid, status="STALLED_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING", last_error="previous status")

        with patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.poller.list_authenticated_user_uploads", return_value=[{"video_id": vid}]), \
             patch("dispatch.youtube_inbox.oauth.get_youtube_processing_status", side_effect=RuntimeError("network timeout")), \
             patch.object(self.catcher, "ingest_video") as mock_ingest:
            self.poller.poll_once()

        mock_ingest.assert_not_called()
        record = db.get_youtube_video(vid)
        self.assertEqual(record["status"], "STALLED_YOUTUBE_PROCESSING")
        self.assertIn("YOUTUBE_STATUS_UNAVAILABLE", record["last_error"])
        self.assertIn("network timeout", record["last_error"])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

============================================================
FILE: Transcript\01_EVERYTHING_you_need_to_know_to_build_a_Dashboard_UI_in_8_minutes_(beginner_friendly).txt
============================================================
Link: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=B7k5rOgmOGY&t=49s&pp=ygUTdWkgZGFzaGJvYXJkIGRlc2lnbg%3D%3D
Title: EVERYTHING you need to know to build a Dashboard UI in 8 minutes (beginner friendly)

Dashboards are a staple of any design portfolio, but most suck. Not because they're ugly, but because they're disorganized and messy. So, in this video, I'm going to show you how to design your very first dashboard UI completely from scratch in a way that's simple, aesthetic, and actually usable. And by the end, you won't just have a great-look layout. You'll understand why the best dashboards feel effortless to use.

It starts with the sidebar, which houses most of the persistent and globally relevant elements. Think about it as the spine of your product. Elements like navigation, profile management and search are typically in here, but sometimes profile management and search can go in the top bar, too. Starting from the top, sometimes a logo is put up here, but I find this a more convenient spot for profile management.

A profile picture makes it easy to identify, and the arrows indicate that this is clickable. Next, links. We want to start with a recognizable icon and a short title. This is especially helpful if you plan on making your sidebar collapsible, like this. And it's also helpful if you want to add a notification number or a new chip to it. Navigation is all about reducing cognitive load. To do that, we'll want to group our links by what's relevant.

And then since settings and help center are rarely used, we can send them down to the bottom. As the amount of links expands, you'll want to start nesting links into drop downs. And make sure to have an active state. We're using this rectangle as an indicator. This is optional, but if you want to highlight features, search or integrations, this would be a good place to put them in.

Finally, also optional, but with all this empty space, we could add in some notifications. Companies like Dub and Linear have already started doing this sort of thing. And just like that, you've built an aesthetic, but also functional sidebar super easily. If it wasn't clear already, we're building a link tracking software for this demo. So, let's get into it. Sidebars are relatively hard to screw up. The same cannot be said about dashboards.

This dashboard feels like someone emptied a drawer onto the screen. If your dashboard looks like it requires a PhD to operate, then it's too complex. Just do one thing well with your dashboard. First, I want to point out a few important considerations. If you compare a dashboard to a landing page, you'll see the features of a dashboard are much smaller because we're trying to shove in many more things.

These are the textiles for the landing page, and these are the textiles for the dashboard. We've got a lot of smaller font sizes without much spacing in between sizes. Grids and layouts are also much more strictly followed for dashboards than landing pages because we're using most or all of the space on screen. Third and final, what you put in this main section of the dashboard reflects what is most important to the user.

For example, if you're building a project management dashboard, displaying the project status would be very useful. Or if you're building a financial dashboard, you should show investments right at the top. Taking all of that into consideration, we'll be using a very simple two column, two row grid with link management at the top and a few important metrics down below. The very top of the dashboard is generally reserved for important page actions or simple navigation.

For our relatively simple dashboard, we'll have a drop down and a button to make a link, which seems pretty important. For any data displayed on the homepage, you'll want to keep it super simple. So, we'll just have the favicon, the shorten link, the actual link, and when it was made. We'll also add how many clicks each link has gotten. Since we still want to have a good amount of space in the middle, we can add a brief description, kind of similar to GitHub.

If we wanted visual separation, we can make each link have its own border. But for less clutter, we'll stack them into a list. This layout also makes it easier to add an empty state when there are no links to display, which is important to be thinking about. Now, this static list is looking great, but in real world applications, we need to manage data efficiently. This is where a small micro interaction comes in.

Letting the user select multiple links which reveals a new contextual button, bulk actions. Moving on to the charts below. Let's do it properly. First, don't make weird like this. I don't know what these are, and neither does anyone else. Instead, let's start with a basic line graph. Add some grid lines and numbers since everyone forgets those. Pop in a quick summary and date selector, and we're good to go.

For the other one, we'll have a bar chart to break down by each link with the favicon on the side to make them easily identifiable. We'll add the same range selector and give the user access to signups and conversion on this chart, too. There's no reason that charts can't be simple and informative, but also aesthetic. But sometimes you need some inspiration first. And one tool I use all the time for this is Mvin, who curates hundreds of thousands of beautifully organized screenshots from real apps.

things like project management tools, banking dashboards, and even admin panels. You can search by element like charts or even filter by industry or device type. And it's not just pretty UIs. It helps me see how real teams design flows like how they onboard users or structure complex settings pages. You can check out Mobin using my link in the description to get 20% off. However, most of the functionality of a dashboard isn't actually here in what we've built.

We're displaying a lot of information, but we can't do anything yet. That's where modals, popovers, and even new pages come into play. A popover is something like this, and best used when the context is simple, like these display settings, and are nonblocking, meaning the user can click away without any consequences. A modal is best used when the context is more complex, but you still want to keep the user on the same page.

This is because creating a link is directly related to the links that are displayed on screen. A modal is considered blocking because you need to click create or cancel before dealing with other things. And since you can't see the page when the changes are being made, a toast notification confirming the changes is generally a good practice. Speaking of toasts, they're the notification system of your dashboard. Anytime you want to make the user aware of something without taking over the entire screen or prompt them to take action, you use a toast.

We talked about empty states earlier, but toasts are great for a warning or error states, which frequently get missed. Finally, if the context is more permanent or is very large, like clicking on an existing link, it's reasonable to direct the user to a new page. If you're going to do that, having a back button or breadcrumb is pretty much required. We've just built the bulk of one page on a dashboard, but laying out other pages are fairly trivial since there are really only four main dashboard components.

First, lists and tables. These are the most common, and we've used several of them already, from our link table to link details or even the sidebar. Making a good list mostly comes down to separation with three ways to do it. The first is just space. The second is lines or dividers. And the third is with color. But making a good table is more about functionality where displaying the data is only half the battle.

Giving the user the options to search, filter, or sort the table transforms it into an interactive tool. Next is cards. This includes our charts and toast notifications. Most dashboards are comprised of many cards together. Keep the margins well spaced so content isn't too tightly packed. Also, you can choose between a border or background colors. I prefer outlines on dark modes and background colors on light mode, but that's totally up to you.

User input is another common one. We've used these making our link modal and any sort of settings pages has them. Or if you're lucky, you'll get tables with forms inside of cards like here on Versel. Fourth and final are tabs. These are fantastic for essentially adding new pages without cluttering the sidebar. For example, in notion, we get different views of very related tables without leaving the context of the page.

Breaking Notion settings down a little more, we've got mostly lists with some user inputs and some cards once we get to connections, all inside of a modal. So once you master these four elements, you can build virtually any dashboard page. Finally, animation and interaction on dashboards is pretty tame and user focused when compared to some websites and landing pages. Starting with our chart animations, here is where we can get a little more creative.

For example, on this chart, when we hover, we get the number as well as a bubble with a percent in it. Or on our other chart, when we hover, all of the other bars dim out. But what everyone wants is a snappy, fast dashboard. That's where optimistic UI comes into play. For example, on Gmail, when I delete an email, it disappears instantly, assuming that the server request will succeed.

The app is optimistic that it will succeed so you don't get these awkward pauses before your link disappears. And with that, you have the majority of a functional aesthetic dashboard, which is more than most designers can say. If you're interested in checking out Mobin, it'll be the first link down below. Title: The 3 dashboard UI flaws that give away you've NEVER built one

Designing a dashboard isn't that hard, but orchestrating one is. And there's a massive difference between UI that might look good and UI that actually works when you start using it. So, whether you're live coding a new project or creating a piece for your portfolio, we're covering the three tells that immediately give away you've never built one before and exactly how to fix each and every one. >> [music] >> The one thing that all dashboards have in common is they're built to display data.

So, if we focus on building the UI around the data we need to display, we end up with a much more usable experience. For example, take a look at this table. It seems relatively logically laid out, but clearly nothing is driving the form. But, for example, if we turned the department and employment into chips or took these numbers and right align them, things start to shape up.

We do this because there's only a set number of departments or employment statuses, and we right align the numbers so the digits align by place value. Then, we can augment this further by truncating long text to give more breathing room to the other columns or even shade out rows of the table that are inactive or deactivated. And when we compare the before and after, we can clearly see which is more visually appealing to look at and importantly, which is letting the data drive the form.

And the same is true for data that probably shouldn't even be in a table in the first place. This table definitely works. Don't get me wrong, we could use it, but the time delineated nature means that this isn't the best format and we aren't letting the data determine the shape of the UI. Instead, if we were to arrange this as a timeline, it starts to become a lot easier to follow rather than a time sorted table.

This form factor could then be tucked into a sidebar pop out or made wider as a second column beside the table, but either way, now we're letting the data drive the UI. And color is a very important consideration when we do start designing these tables. [music] Landing pages, you can get away with a lot, but dashboard colors shouldn't just be sprinkled in to look nice. It should come from the data itself.

In these activity logs, the red icon in the chip draws your eye to it because the action is urgent. The avatar is there because your eye can associate who did what way faster than reading a name in the column. And once you're thinking that way, it's a pretty simple jump to adding a chart to roll up the data into a summary. Because this data has a time dimension, and our chart shows you that instantly instead of making you hunt through a timestamp column, just like Often when I talk about hierarchy, I use examples like this, where this card has poor hierarchy, and this card has strong hierarchy.

But there's another type of hierarchy based on what you show and what you hide, and it's called progressive disclosure. For example, this share functionality isn't permanently ingrained in the side of the table since it's not that frequently accessed and frankly not important enough. Instead, we tuck it into a popover so we don't have to rip the user to an entirely different page. And within this popover, the primary action is of course the search box, which is why it's immediately visible at the top.

A secondary action like removing a user isn't overly important, so instead of ingraining it in the UI, we show the remove button on hover along with a tooltip. We could be more explicit by always having them, or even still more explicit by having the full label present. But since we're tight on space and removing a user from sharing isn't a main action, we progressively reveal it as soon as the user starts to look for that functionality.

And what I've just described is called the spectrum of explicitness, where a global, always visible share button is quite high, whereas an icon to copy a cell on hover is much lower on that same spectrum. And Apple does the same thing really well in their Reminders app. On swipe left and right, it reveals useful but secondary actions compared to actually checking off the reminder. And if you think about it, this is exactly what an onboarding is.

When a user lands on your app for the first time, they shouldn't be staring at a fully loaded dashboard wondering where to start. A good onboarding might start with a single tooltip pointing at the most important action. Once they've done that, maybe a second one appears or a simple checklist in the corner. You're not hiding functionality, you're sequencing it so that the user is never overwhelmed. Compare that to explaining the entire product in a modal with six bullet points the second you log in and then instantly forgetting the moment you dismiss.

And the best way to get a feel for how to handle this, the progressive disclosure, the spectrum of explicitness, and the onboarding flows, is to look at a lot of real software. If you're interested in checking it out, the very first link A really important concept that AI has a hard time wrapping its figurative head around is that UI is as much about what you can see as what you can't see.

Like for as much as this table works well and looks good, additional functionality in such a dense design needs to come from parts of the UI that you can't immediately see. This could be as simple as a copy chip over the cells or perhaps comment functionality with a small triangle indicator. Here's all the UI that you can see and then this is all of the UI that you can't immediately see but is still there.

And it's not a small amount. We need it all to actually make any of this table function. Putting the individual pieces together, the spacing, the sizing isn't that hard but orchestrating them does require some thought. And this applies just as much to the smaller items as it does to the larger drawers, models, and elements that have hidden components and states. It makes it easier to see that new features or elements often don't require a dedicated page just to be better thought through how they're implemented.

But, this isn't just true for dense UI like tables. Think about moments in software that you almost never see. An announcement about a newly launched feature or an onboarding pop-up explaining how part of the product works. But, what's missing pretty much unequivocally on all beginner dashboards are tooltips. We have to assume that the user won't understand all of our icons or that they might want more details or information on ambiguous labels.

It goes to show that there's so much UI that's hiding that's important for a finished product. FILE: Transcript\05_The_secret_behind_weirdly_perfect_UI_designs.txt
